ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-07-senat-31-1.u2	Dear Marshal, Dear Friend! Dear Senators! Dear Colleagues and Dear Colleagues! I'm a parliamentarians myself, so I feel at home here. If I may say so. Ladies and gentlemen, please allow me to begin by thanking you. Thank you for letting me speak in the Senate of the Republic of Poland as President of the Parliamentary Assembly of the Council of Europe. But on this occasion I would also like to share a few more personal reflections. I am pleased that the Polish Parliament is still functioning and that it is still performing its democratic functions, despite the challenges that the COVID-19 pandemic poses to all of us. Because it is important to remember that parliaments still have to perform their function in times of pandemic, in particular by representing citizens, adopting laws and exercising control over executive power. And this is one of the main tasks. I would like to stress that no executive authority can have full freedom, it must be controlled by people elected by citizens, i.e. by the Sejm and the Senate. It seems to me that a kind of red flag is if this constant control of Parliament over executive power is disturbed. The assembly I represent here is one of the two main bodies of the Council of Europe The second is the Committee of Ministers. It is a real and important platform for inter-parliamental dialogue, as well as parliamentary diplomacy, as it brings together representatives of 47 Member States, but also representatives of observer states and countries with a partner status for democracy. As parliamentarians, we therefore represent the whole spectrum of political life. What is important to us, to the Parliamentary Assembly, is that we really represent everyone. This is a characteristic that distinguishes us from the Committee of Ministers, because the Committee of Ministers is, of course, a representative of the government supported by the majority. Now a little digression on the Council of Europe, for those who are not 100% familiar with the subject. The Council of Europe is an organisation based on the European Convention on Human Rights, established 70 years ago on fundamental freedoms and fundamental rights. I think you must understand, Mr Marshal and Dear colleagues, that the European Convention on Human Rights should be seen as a kind of constitution, a European constitution of common values. This means that we all live in a single legal area that protects the rights of more than 850 million citizens living in these 47 Member States. It also means And I'd like to stress that very strongly. Because we are bound by the European Convention on Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms, which is the basis of our work, we also have a court, i.e. Court of Human Rights in Strasbourg. And in this single legal area, the ruling of this Court must be applied by all Member States. I would like to make it clear here, because sometimes there are misunderstandings and thoughts that the European Convention on Human Rights can be applied freely. No, it's not. This is a constitution which is superior to all other constitutions, which defines our true values, human rights and fundamental freedoms: mentioned in the article. 2 the right to life referred to in Article. 6 the right to a fair trial, referred to in other articles, equality, freedom of expression, freedom of religion and other rights. That is what I wanted to start with, because sometimes there are misunderstandings and thinking that the Council of Europe is an entity that did not create a single legal space. Because he created, as a result of the adoption of the European Convention on Human Rights and the acceptance of its validity, we all live in a single common legal space. One of the important lessons that the pandemic has taught us is that unilateral human actions do not provide stable, sufficient and effective solutions. We live in an increasingly globalized and interconnected world vessel system, and confrontation with the tendencies of fragmentation and isolation, because that's what triggered the pandemic. She forced all our countries to turn to the inside. This has harmed the multilateral approach to real problems. But now it's important that we all and each one of us Whatever groups we come from, they supported multilateral relations and joint efforts in solving global problems. And that's our goal, that's what we offer and that's what we want to inspire. I'm going to use the COVID example. We all know that COVID-19 doesn't stop at any border. On the contrary, what happens in one country affects the situation in other countries. And this is proof that multilateral action is the right way to solve cross-border problems such as COVID etc. So the standard of multilateralism of cooperation adopted in the Council of Europe is the basis for what needs to be done. We can't do this on our own. We have to work together. In recent months, the Parliamentary Assembly has organised a number of meetings on current issues and topics that are important across Europe. Each of these debates has brought very concrete conclusions. We've been looking into things like this. COVID-19 and its impact on our citizens, we have analysed and signaled to the Committee of Ministers very important issues related to artificial intelligence and its impact on democracy, such as fake news, because it relates to the issue of artificial intelligence, and we have raised an extremely serious issue of combating violence against women, because this problem has unfortunately become much worse during the pandemic. Last week, during the autumn session, the Parliamentary Assembly also focused on many environmental problems. Thank you for that. - Thank you, too. - Thank you. All of you, not only my colleagues in Poland, but also the representatives of all 47 Member States, because we unanimously adopted 7 important reports that show the need to change the paradigms within national and international laws, as well as in government strategies, to ensure that a healthy environment is treated as a fundamental human right. It is not just the essence of this problem that is important here. We know that in our society it is young people who are extremely sensitive to these It's a good thing you've seen it. The problems, so if these recommendations that we have adopted unanimously give us a chance, then this is it. also an opportunity for all of us, including those who are no longer young, to reach the young part of society and to do something together. This is, so far, one of the first moments when I see a problem that we can face together. So environmental protection, a very important issue for the future of all of us, is also an issue that can reconnect us all and help rebuild relations with young people. And I would like to say here in the Senate, as I would say in every parliament of 47 Member States, that this is the way it needs to be decorated. We must go to young people and say: we, as the Council of Europe, say that a healthy environment is a fundamental human right, and we approach it unanimously. Policy in this area has been and continues to be For me, but also for others, an extremely important issue in the area of interest of the Parliamentary Assembly of the Council of Europe. Thanks to the partnership that started when you joined the Council of Europe in 1991, i.e. about 30 years ago, Senate and Sejm representatives contributed to many interesting and constructive discussions and other activities of the Parliamentary Assembly, which helped promote fundamental human rights, democracy and the rule of law in Europe and beyond. Poland and its representatives at our assembly should definitely appreciate the many important achievements that are a model for other countries. As regards specific examples of such achievements, not only from the distant past, but also from this recent one, during the last 2 days of meetings I had in Warsaw, I had the opportunity to become a witness to them and learn about many facts that confirm this thesis. An example is that Poland was one of the first countries to ratify a widely accepted, most effective, advanced legal tool for combating and ending violence against women and domestic violence, known as the Istanbul Convention. I would like to stress very clearly that this is a tool that is specifically aimed at combating violence against women. And I would like to say to everyone here that this is not a tool for any other purpose. I know there's some sensitivity to gender issues, but the Istanbul Convention, which is the tool I'm talking about, was created only to combat violence against women. And by extending my statement, I want everyone who thinks that this tool can be abused and used for other purposes, to ask very much that they do not preach it, because, in the end, it undermines the fight against violence against women. I welcome the fact that some of you know the Grevio monitoring body, and that it presented its report 3 weeks ago. He stressed there, dear colleagues, very many very positive achievements which confirm the fact that Polish politicians and, more importantly, society see the clear importance and added value of the multilateral approach to this scandalous problem. We all know from our background the cases of people who commit violence against women. And we should all fight this phenomenon together. We can't do this alone, we should unite for this. Of course, something can be improved everywhere, not only in Poland, but also in my country, but let us focus on the real issue, which is the fight against violence against women. Of course, in many countries, including Poland, there is a need to stop sexual violence, rape, sexual harassment. But maybe we should start by looking at the definition of rape as what is laid down in the conventions. That is why I have encouraged your marshal and fellow MPs to submit this debate and also to read the recommendations of the GREVIO report and to think about how we can build and strengthen a legal and political framework to provide women with better protection against violence. The Convention maintains the strong role of national Parliaments in this field, and this is quite unique because the Istanbul Convention is one of those conventions which assigns a particular role to national Parliaments to play, which is why I believe it is time to raise these issues in the Senate, as it must be raised in the parliaments of all 47 Member States of the Council of Europe. Violence against women is deeply rooted in existing inequalities between women and men, in systematic discrimination against women. It exists, we can't be blind to it. Therefore, this issue requires a comprehensive national strategy for equality between women and men, which, in my opinion, is an extremely important element to face violence against women. And speaking of equality between men and women, we need to see this subject as a whole. And here's a short digression. Sometimes I get the feeling that some of our colleagues think equality means being the same. No, absolutely not. Equality means whoever we are, whatever our beliefs are, we are equal in rights. So the right to equality, perceived from the right angle, dear colleagues, is the right to be different. That's what equality means. Thus, in the context of the European Convention on Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms, every citizen, each individual has the right to be different and has the right to respect. All of us and each one of us should have respect for each other, regardless of our views. Now, of course, it's an issue here and in other countries. LGBTI community rights and other rights. This is a sensitive subject and it is constantly emerging, together with other issues, at the heart of the broad interest of the Council of Europe. It's true. Yeah, we have to be honest with each other. The image of Poland is suffering because of these zones free from LGBTI. I have heard many explanations about this and today I understand the situation much better than two days ago. However, it is true that this does not change the way Poland is perceived. But I would also like to add that I have seen very positive achievements of the Polish government and I would like to express my appreciation for this, because, as I understand from our discussions, measures have been taken to remove any discriminatory or perceived as discriminatory records from documents. And even if I know it now The documents have no legal value and no binding force, and I was very pleased to learn that five regions have already complied with our requests for this matter. Let me repeat for the sake of clarity: this is not about family values and any rights in opposition to them, including the rights of LGBTI people. We just have to respect each other, dear colleagues and Mr. Marshal. That's what this is all about. I will say again: the right to equality is, in its essence, the right to be different, but at the same time to be respected and to be able to show respect for others. This is the core of what this right to be different means. I hope so and I will encourage all of you to support strong legislation against hatred and discrimination in Poland and to ensure that it is effectively implemented. Political leaders in all countries are responsible for countering all forms of hatred, including prejudice against LGBTI people and these, as we know, exist among the population. We know it exists, I'm not blind, you're not blind, we know it. But just because we see it and know it doesn't mean we don't have to fight it. I repeat: we must protect the right of every citizen to be different and to be respected and to respect others. Poland is not only an important member of the Council of Europe. I, Mr Marshal, dear colleagues, believe that your country has great potential to be one of the leading European countries, and even as far as I am concerned, to be a leader in the region. I'm pretty sure about that. I felt that way last week, and now, after the talks I had with important politicians in your country, I am even more convinced of this. E.g. The Warsaw Security Forum, on which I had the pleasure of speaking yesterday, shows a broad appreciation for the role that Poland plays in the region. And it's important not to jeopardize this potential to be the leader you have as a country. I'm moving on to another issue that's also up to date. And we know that. It was said that some legal reforms in Poland had a very negative impact on the independence of the judiciary and respect for the rule of law in Poland. This had a negative impact on the functioning of democratic institutions in the country. This ultimately caused our assembly to open the so-called. the monitoring procedure in this case and, as you know, the European courts have given their judgments on this matter. But I would like to stress one thing here. The assembly and its rapporteurs, other actors, including the Venice Commission, have never questioned and will not question the need or the right of the authorities of any country, including Poland, to make changes, to undertake reforms. Let me repeat: I stress that the assembly and its rapporteur, as well as many other actors, including the Venice Commission, do not question the need and the right of the authorities or parliament to reform the judiciary in the case of any country, including Poland. Nevertheless, it must be stressed that such reforms must respect and strengthen the independence of the judiciary and the rule of law, not act against them. This is related to Article. 6th European Convention on Human Rights, Mr Marshal, and this is a binding convention and we must follow it. If this is not the case, it may contribute to the further negative perception of Poland. I want to counteract this negative image of Poland. But it is true that now, in an important dimension, it seems, the rule of law is being undermined and threatened. I think that you, the Senate, the Sejm, Members of Parliament, should improve this if the amendments are needed. As Parliamentary Assembly, the Council of Europe as a whole, we are ready to help you, but not because we want to interfere, on the contrary, we simply have the experience of 46 other countries, maybe better, maybe worse experiences, but we can give you our expertise so that you can make democratic decisions on how to actually build the rule of law in your own country. I would also like to say that being criticized is normal. I'm being criticized, too. But criticism can be viewed in a positive way as helping to create a better society And you're welcome to take it that way. I will repeat here again: we, as Council of Europe, will be on the spot, we are ready to support you, we will provide expertise without interfering in how you will make decisions, but all of this must be in compliance with the European Convention on Human Rights. If this is how it will be done, Mr. Marshal, dear colleagues from the Senate, then we will see good leadership of Poland in the region. Because we need you. We as Council of Europe need a Polish contribution to promoting democracy and human rights in the region and beyond. We need you guys to make this clear. One more thing. Poland is now facing a very difficult situation at the border with Belarus. This is, in my opinion, a scandalous situation. The fact that there are these hybrid attacks by the Belarusian authorities, which, as we seem to think, result in increasing migration. This is one of the issues that have been discussed most intensively during my stay in your beautiful country, especially in the last 2 days. This is also a question that may cause further negative information. But this also shows great opportunities to reverse the situation and start procedures based on coherent, joint issues, regional and European cooperation. As you know or you do not know, last week our assembly as a result of the initiative of Polish parliamentarians and of the Sejm, and of the Senate, of both majority, if I may say so, because also of the ruling party, and of the opposition Once again, I would like to stress this: it was the united front of parliamentarians from Poland that adopted a resolution on the instrumentalised migratory pressure at the border between Latvia, Lithuania and Poland and Belarus. This was a unanimous and unambiguous condemnation of this situation, the core of which is increased artificially, politically controlled, illegal trafficking in human beings carried out and organised by the Belarusian authorities with a total violation of human rights and the dignity of migrants and refugees. The political purpose of this is destabilization. And the sad aspect of this case is suffering people suffering human beings. How to solve this problem, taking into account 2 different perspectives, i.e. How can we combine the fight for these people with the failure of instrumentalisation by a regime that looks at Europe as a whole with an unfavorable eye? Clearly, the political situation in Belarus and its lack of respect for international humanitarian law and human rights can no longer be seen, in my opinion, in terms of the internal affairs of any country, because it is not such a matter. This must be seen as something that is a serious complication for the whole of Europe. Poland, like the other countries mentioned above, is bordering countries, but it is a European issue, it is not just a national issue. By the way, the dignity of man and helping suffering people is not a national issue either, it is a universal question of the value of human dignity. And this is how we should all treat it, of course, taking into account the political implications that are at stake here. So this is about combining issues in the context of regional and European cooperation. That is how I see it, but this is also the general view, because this resolution was adopted unanimously, above the divisions, as I said, at the initiative of Polish parliamentarians. One of them, Alex, is here with us. And that's what we said there. And that's what I'm going to propose. That's the main message of my speech. As you know, the PACE resolution includes many proposals to address this complex problem in a practical but also effective way. Poland, which is on the front line in this growing migration crisis, should play a leading role here, both with the assembly and with the Council of Europe as a whole, in order to encourage other European countries to engage and participate. Because it's not a one-sided problem, it's a multilateral issue. This resolution, unanimously supported by the entire Polish delegation to the Parliamentary Assembly, expresses Poland's commitment to uphold human rights and the principles of humanitarianism as a right concerning refugees and their right to seek asylum, but in accordance with the principles of multilateralism and joint action by all concerned, on which the European community is based. Moreover, the resolution calls on the Belarusians Although I should say: the Belarusian authorities To act in accordance with accepted international obligations, in particular the 1951 Geneva Convention. Let me add that we're celebrating right now. Although I do not know whether it is appropriate to define the 70th anniversary of the signing of the Geneva Convention, which applies to all of us, including Belarus. I am convinced that the initiative that comes from the Parliamentary Assembly of the Council of Europe and has the full support of all the political forces of Poland, as we saw in the Parliamentary Assembly, that your Senate, Mr Marshal, together with the Sejm, can play an important role in this context. That's what I told my callers yesterday and the day before yesterday. And today I would like to propose that all political forces in Poland, the Sejm, the Senate, but also we, as Parliamentary Assembly, turn to the Council of Europe as an institution and work together, in multilateral relations, to solve this terrible problem, in order to protect the human dignity and life of asylum seekers, because this is what the Geneva Convention requires of us, but at the same time we should all act multilaterally to solve the political problem which is the result of this situation. This is, I stress, my message to each and every one of you here in the Senate. I believe, Mr. Marshal, that we should act together in this matter. And I hope that in the coming days we will be able to make progress in dealing with this terrible issue. Thank you very much for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-07-senat-31-1.u9	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Committee on Budget and Public Finance a report on the work of our committee on amending the Budget Law for 2021. The first report is contained in print No 519 A and the second in print No 520 A. Mr Marshal, High Izbo, the Committee on Budget and Public Finance took note of these two important laws yesterday, analyzed them and discussed them. We have worked through these 2 laws and we propose that the High House accept the amendments that are the result of our work. To amend the budget law This is in the report in print No 519 A We propose the introduction of one amendment, which is, in effect, talking about the priorities of the committee, about the most important issues that we believe should be the subject of our greater commitment. It is about allocating additional resources to protect health. We propose to increase expenditure in part 46 of the health section 85195 by PLN 6 billion 300 million. I am not going to talk more about the justification for this expenditure. I think that in the debate the senators will pay more attention to this problem, to this priority and why we are talking about health in amending the law for 2021. The Act amending the budget law for 2021 was passed by the Sejm last Friday and was sent to the Senate on Monday. The Marshal, in reply to the Minister of Finance's request, agreed that both laws should be examined at this Senate meeting. The Marshal's decision is that we realise how important these laws are. Budgetary laws are always important because they concern state expenditure, state revenue, as well as the financing of various important public objectives. Therefore, the answer and consent of Marshal Grodzki to this request was obvious, and we immediately made an effort to examine this bill and report today. During our meeting, Mr Skuz of the Ministry of Finance first presented, discussed the bill, its objectives, the revision of the budget law for 2021, and then the senators raised questions and discussed all the doubts that arose during the discussions. Why the Law for 2021 is - No, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, The obvious reasons are that the revenue of the state budget has increased significantly in the second half of the year. The increase in this revenue compared to the planned revenue in the budget law for 2021 is clear and amounts to PLN 78.5 billion. This appropriation is intended to cover the various objectives which arise during the financial year. We in the discussion drew attention to the fundamental problem and asked why these budgetary resources were so large, where the additional amounts in the state budget came from at the end of the year. Of course, some of the things that have contributed to the fact that these revenues have increased so clearly, we have been paying attention in the Senate, also in our committee, during the work on the budget for 2021. We have pointed out in a fundamental and decisive manner, showing that tax revenues are clearly underestimated, that much higher revenue should be included in the budget law, because this was also indicated by the trends in economic development that occurred at the end of last year and at the beginning of this year, as well as the projected increases in budgetary revenue. So at the time, we thought that tax revenues were clearly underestimated. That's one thing. Two. We pointed out that the income from the National Bank of Poland, which was estimated at PLN 1 billion 300 million, should also be much higher, that this contribution to the state budget should be much higher and several times higher than that planned in the state budget. Therefore, our committee also asked the President of the National Bank of Poland to inform us how the income of the National Bank of Poland is actually estimated. We got a very evasive answer to that question, and we didn't actually get an answer. Our estimates, estimates that we have accepted, and we assumed that this increase should be at a minimum of PLN 3 and PLN 4 billion, and yet the Committee on Budget and Public Finance does not have such apparatus as the Ministry of Finance, as the National Bank of Poland, was much better, more correct than those prepared by the National Bank of Poland and the Ministry of Finance. The third issue that has clearly contributed to revenue growth is inflation. We've been paying attention to this problem, too. The budget law for 2021 includes an inflation target of 1.8%. Today we all know that inflation is definitely higher and that annual inflation will be at least 4,8%. Today, inflation is above 5%. Of course, the rise in inflation also increases revenue to the state budget, in particular the increase in VAT revenue, because any increase in prices automatically results in an increase in VAT, in value, in percentage, in value, it is always a significant increase in value. Revenues from the sale of CO2 allowances have also increased significantly. This amount is also a dozen billion. These are revenues, which were difficult to predict at the beginning of the year, but the trend in price increases for emission allowances was clearly greater than expected in the Budget Act. So these revenues that have emerged and which we think should be largely planned in the budget for 2021... Well, these reasons for the increase in revenue were very much. This may also partly indicate that it is budget planning, financial planning is quite weak in the Ministry of Finance or that it is a deliberate procedure aimed at underestimating these revenues and underestimating the indicators taken into account when estimating the budget for the following years. As additional revenues appeared, the Ministry of Finance decided to amend the Budget Act, assuming that the measures which appeared as additional to the State budget should be earmarked for several important purposes. These objectives are clearly visible in this revision of the Budget Law. The Ministry of Finance proposes in the Act that a penny of funds be earmarked for support to local governments. In total, the amount planned for support to local governments for lost tax revenue, and in the past and in the future... The proposal of the Minister of Finance concerns a total of PLN 12 billion, except that 8 billion is due to lost budget revenue and 4 billion is funds for investments in municipalities for sewage, waterworks, other infrastructure investments. In addition, there is an increase in resources to support the local government in the implementation of investments covering municipal and district roads. Significant measures are also foreseen for the modernisation of railway lines, the extension of railway lines. These are the funds that will be used to support the Railway Fund and the Road Fund. In this amendment, significant resources were allocated to increase the operational capacity of the Armed Forces, to purchase and modernise in the army. The amount foreseen in this budgetary amendment is above PLN 6 billion. In this amendment of the Budget Act, the funds were also transferred to investment tasks of the Police Office, Border Guard, State Fire Service. An important expenditure, which is also included in this revised budget law, is the funds transferred to the incentive fund for the staff of the budgetary sphere. In the budget, the total amount for this is PLN 2.7 billion. Measures have also been earmarked to increase expenditure on financing cultural, agricultural, hard coal mining tasks. It is important that the budget law for 2021 clearly reduces the state budget deficit projected at the end of the year. This deficit would almost double to PLN 41.8 billion. We, as the Committee on Budgets and Public Finance, are always pleased with the measures to reduce the deficit, to reduce debt, but we are aware that it is probably difficult to avoid budget deficits especially in situations such as those we have been dealing with recently, so it is about COVID and various difficult problems with the functioning of the economy. Ladies and gentlemen, during the discussion on the revision of the budget law, we asked first of all why the budget law is planned in such a way that it needs to be amended and so far gone. It was one of the most fundamental dilemmas that we discussed during the meeting of the Committee on Budgets and Public Finance. The second very important point that we raised was the question of increasing the resources that are so-called. non-extinguishables. Practice so far... That is, the Public Finance Act assumes that non-extinguishing measures, i.e. those which are at the disposal of individual budget managers, should be spent and settled by the end of March of the following year, but in practice this period has been extended in recent years until the end of November of the following year. And we have been paying attention to this problem in a fundamental way, because in practice this means that in a large part of the budget is de facto a 2-year budget. Since a large part of the 2020 budget is spent by the end of November 2021. And that's what's expected and likely to happen next year. In practice, it can be said that this budget is being spent in the following years. As we have pointed out, this means that an increasing proportion of the funds are outside Parliament's control and that spending them is illegible and incomprehensible to the public. The government is obliged to present by the end of May a report on the implementation of the budget law for the previous year, so that such a report can also present those non-extinguishable measures which were spent by the end of March. However, the funds that are spent outside of this time are no longer shown in the funded report. We also talked about this on the occasion of the around budget law. And we have therefore proposed an amendment to make these measures visible in the government's financial statements, so that this information is passed on to Parliament and the Supreme Audit Chamber. During the discussions on the Budgetary Law, we also drew attention to some problems related to, for example, with funds spent on the payroll fund and a 6% prize fund. It will also be non-extinguishable measures, quite significant amounts. And the question that actually arose after our committee meeting, but perhaps it is worth asking this question again here. It is about how these funds are distributed, about how trade unions will have an impact on the allocation of these funds. Previously, it was agreed with trade unions to allocate these funds, and now there is doubt as to whether it would be merely an opinion by trade unions on the funds allocated to this prize fund. I hope the Minister will answer that question as well. Due to the fact that quite significant resources were allocated to various local government projects, there was also a problem regarding the financing of support for local authorities. The point is that in this ratio of the allocation of funds per municipality, determined depending on the problems that occur within the municipalities concerned, also to take into account those municipalities of the Senate already several times, which are park municipalities, i.e. those in which there are national parks. The Minister has undertaken to provide more detailed information on this subject. We hope that this issue will also be resolved and agreed after years. So much for this law on amending the budget law. In turn, to the Act on amending the Law on Special Solutions for the Implementation of the Budget Law for 2021 and the Act on Income of Local Government Units, we propose some amendments. They're in our print. The most important amendment from the perspective of the Committee on Budgets and Public Finance is the amendment which obliges the government to report on the implementation of the budget on the non-extinguishable measures by the end of January of the following year and to forward this report to the Sejm and the Supreme Audit Chamber. The same shall apply to the submission of a report on the implementation of the entire budget. The Minister has accepted this amendment. There have been some doubts about this issue, but we hope that the practice of reporting on the expenditure of these unexploited funds will contribute to the fact that the state budget will be more transparent, clearer. In the context of the debate on the non-extinguishable measures we mentioned here, we also said that we were increasingly feeling that the budget should not be a one-year budget, as is apparent from the Public Finance Act, so that it would become more and more a long-term budget, for a period of two years or even longer. It is possible that future work on such a budget should be undertaken The same is true in the European Union But this really requires a very solid planning, much more solid than you can see in this law for 2021 and its amendment. Well, here you can see that they are unfortunately quite divergent. There has been a change in relation to those plans from the previous year. It is possible that such multiannual planning should be gradually transformed into a financial plan, an annual expenditure plan for a given year. In order to allow a more smooth and flexible functioning of the State. This case was taken at a committee meeting, but the minister did not comment on it. It's obvious that this requires a different discussion, other conversations. An amendment was also tabled during the discussion on the Law amending the Budget Law for 2021... And there's a minority request to adopt a budget law without amendments, but that's what the minority rapporteur will already talk about. Thank you very much, Mr. Marshal.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-07-senat-31-1.u90	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! The Senate Marshal referred the income law of local government units and some other laws to 2 committees, to the Committee on Budgets and Public Finances and to the Committee on Territorial Government and State Administration. The Act is related to the Polish Load programme and is intended to reduce the negative effects on the income of local government units. It uses certain tools that increase the revenue of local government units, including: a one-off subsidy to be paid to local government units this year. It is intended to stabilise the income of self-government units with a reference amount, which is, as a rule, equivalent to the annual income of PIT and CIT tax units, i.e. income tax on natural and legal persons. This reference amount is to come from the State budget. Development subsidy is also a new mechanism. There is also a periodic relaxation of the discipline of public finances, which boils down to the possibility of budgeting with a deficit, i.e. with a lower current income than the amount of current expenditure planned. In the committees the discussion lasted quite a long time. It was detailed, sometimes turbulent. Let me brief you on some of the threads of this discussion. Namely, it is important to convert own income into transfers from the budget. Own income was a share that shaped the revenue of local government units more than it would have been after that change, and the compensation was to be made by means of such transfers. This is a limitation on self-reliance. The problem that has also been raised is how to value the reference amount. Namely, in the new idea, this valorization is based on GDP growth. To date, the increase in PIT has translated into the income of local government units and historically increased PIT revenues much faster than they can grow now when they are linked to the gross domestic product. The issue of incomplete compensation was also raised, which is contrary to Article. 167 of the Constitution, and the lack of compensation for lost income as a result of changes in taxes in 2019 and 2020. Another was the planned role of development aid, which is now treated as compensation by the local authorities who notified this idea. It was a self-government initiative. Following such changes, the committees propose to adopt a law with amendments. These amendments are legislative in nature, they were proposed by the Senate Legislative Office. Most of these amendments have been agreed, i.e. they have been accepted by Minister Skuz present in the Chamber, but not all of them. It was not accepted by the Minister, but was accepted by the committee, e.g. an amendment to the vocabulary. One of the amendments that were tabled by the senators and adopted by the majority of the joint committees also concerns the definition, or vocabulary. The aim is to ensure that local governments do not reduce their income as a result of the movement of PIT taxpayers to flat-rate income tax. The Commission has also introduced an increase in the share of local government units in personal income tax. This is calculated in relation to the base for the year 2022, i.e. after reductions, and this is expected to offset the losses in sum. Also this amount planned to be transferred to local government units this year It was treated as compensation for what the local authorities would lose as a result of the introduction of regulations related to the Polish Ład. It was treated as compensation for lost revenue due to tax changes in the years 2019 to 2021. If this were to be the case, this compensation should not amount to 8 billion, but to 12.4 billion. The latest amendment proposed by the joint committees is to increase development aid to the amount of VAT on investments carried out by local government units. This was the original idea that this really impacting VAT on the state budget, charged on local investment, would return to the local government as an investment incentive and support for those self-governments that do not invest, because they could not even afford this start, i.e. they have zero operating surplus and cannot invest. This also increases the amount that was planned to be above PLN 3 billion. The effect of VAT on local investment to the State budget is significantly higher. To conclude, I would like to say that the committees are asking for the bill to be adopted together with the amendments that have been included in the committee's report. Thank you very much.

The Act also provides for a reduction in the threshold for the participation of the European Union in the financing of programmes, projects or tasks financed from Union funds from which the exemption from the individual debt ratio applies. And there would be a reduction from the present 60%. up to 50%. That's a very good solution. It increases the predictability and planning capacity of the budget by the local government unit. This part of the general subsidy, development subsidy, although its name may suggest that it is intended and can only be used for investment, property expenditure, does not lose the quality of the subsidy and is the income of the current local government unit. So this part of the general subsidy can be used to balance the current budget. How would this subsidy be calculated? The basic part of the subsidy, i.e. 60% , would be divided against the number of persons resident in the local government unit and 40% ... here, you could say, the competence criteria of local government units. There would be competition, competition, as 20% of the subsidies would be granted to local government units where investments per capita are higher than the average in a given basket of local government units. Next 20% would be granted in such a system a little competitive as a bonus for those local government units, which annually show the highest progress and are above average. This amount would amount to a minimum of PLN 3 billion. From 2023 onwards, it would be valued annually at the growth rate of the nominal gross domestic product, i.e. taking into account real economic growth and price increases. By means of development grants, the local government unit would receive funds to balance the current budget. In addition, this system of calculation and operation of development subsidies would be, you might say, incorporated into the system of guaranteeing a certain amount of resources to local government units for participation in personal income tax and corporate income tax. Ms Marshal, High Izbo, local government units would be guaranteed that their shares in CIT and PIT, their income from this will not be lower than those calculated from the same titles on the basis of the so-called. the reference amount. They defined the expected receipts for participation in PIT and CIT before the announcement of the Polish Load program, i.e. before the reform of income. This amount would be valued with the increase in nominal gross domestic product, i.e. taking into account price increases but also economic growth. If the amount foreseen for local government units for a given financial year for participation in income taxes was lower than the reference amount, the government would be obliged to increase the development subsidy in the draft budget law by that amount. ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-07-senat-31-1.u110	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I met with self-government officials and I was vice-president of the Lubuskie Sejm, and I had contact with them, just like Senator Wadim Tyszkiewicz, and as part of the preparation for work on the bill under investigation at this point, we discussed what expectations are for the government of the Republic of Poland. And I have to say, there were different answers. They differed slightly in detail, but they can be summarized in one sentence in general: for the inhabitants we want to be an effective host, and for the government we want to be a partner. Why am I talking about this? Because when I went to talk about this law, which is crucial for them, I heard at once that they were afraid that as a result of the changes in the tax system they would not be the first, and the latter did not feel even at the stage of the work on this law. Here we certainly need to say a few sentences about the current situation of local governments in our country. Some of my interlocutors have not yet fully recovered from the effects of education reform, others have drawn attention to current problems, e.g. increasing prices of construction products, thus exceeding the investment cost assumptions resulting from cost estimates, or e.g. wage pressure from their employees, which they understand in principle, knowing the growing in Poland, unfortunately, I have to say this with regret, inflation. Some of my guests, the more ambitious ones, spoke about the preparations being made for the absorption of European Union aid under the new financial perspective, which requires both staff preparation and spending on programming and building work. That's what the self-governments from my Lubusian land said. Now let me change my perspective and draw the attention of the ladies and gentlemen of the senators to what includes the international obligations of our country. The Council of Europe adopted the European Charter of Local Self-Government, which was ratified by Poland. I would like to mention some of the provisions of the article. 9, which speaks of the financial resources of local communities. The local Communities have the right, within the framework of national economic policy, to have sufficient resources of their own which they can freely possess in the exercise of their powers. The financial resources of local communities should be proportionate to the competences provided for by the constitution or law, and the financial systems on which the principles of local communities are based should be sufficiently diversified and evolutionary in order to allow them to stick to, where possible in practice, the real evolution of the costs of exercising their competences. Local communities should be properly consulted on how to allocate resources to them. As it turns out from my discussions with local authorities and from the analysis of the provisions of the European Charter for Local Government, the proposed provisions on the one hand raise legitimate concerns of local authorities, and on the other hand, if they are not in clear contradiction with our international obligations, they are incompatible with their spirit. Analyzing the proposed changes in the financing of local governments, I have serious discomfort, because the function of law, as I often say, is to make life more predictable. However, local authorities have no such belief in the proposed provisions, although our country has committed itself to the fact that local authorities will have adequate funding to carry out their tasks. Is it possible to correct the proposed provisions? In my opinion, yes. Can they be changed in a simple way to make the functioning of local governments more predictable? Here, too, my answer is yes. This can, of course, be achieved by correcting the proposals sent to us and introducing provisions to ensure that the income of municipalities, powiats and voivodships and metropolitan unions will not be reduced as a result of changes in the tax system. The proposed change in the project presented to us will make the life of local governments more predictable than the development subsidy introduced by the Act. The new subsidy must not replace stable local government revenues and may complement their funding system, especially between the end of one and the launch of another European Union budgetary perspective, provided, of course, that it is fairly distributed. Finally, Mrs. Marshal, three amendments. I'm after consulting the Legislative Bureau that's preparing them right now. Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-07-senat-31-1.u112	Thank you, Mrs. Marshal. High Izbo! At both committee meetings, the initiative was accepted, accepted and even well assessed. I hope that this reading will be similar. The Act I am talking about amends the excise duty Act of 6 December 2008. The purpose of the Act is to introduce a regulation allowing the continuation of the activities carried out in the tax warehouse in the event that the former tax warehouse operator resigns from the activity without having to complete the suspension procedure for excise goods in that tax warehouse. The proposed regulation responds to the expectations of entities operating in the form of a tax warehouse. It should be pointed out that they indicate that there is no possibility for another entity to continue its activities in tax formation, which is not the legal successor of the existing tax warehouse operator. These difficulties concern actually all, but especially tax warehouses of the type of production. The current arrangements constitute a barrier to economic activity because, in accordance with the Act of 6 December 2008 on excise duty, the change of the tax warehouse operator requires de facto a new authorisation to operate the warehouse, of course, excluding the situations set out in the Act which relate exclusively to tax successions. In other cases, the change in the tax warehouse operator requires the withdrawal of the excise licence to the existing tax warehouse operator and the issue of a new excise permit to the new entity. All of this causes a de facto break in business. As I said, if this is a continuous activity, in fact it not only makes it difficult, but even prevents it from doing so. I could also give you a lot of details about this law and the solutions it proposes, but I do not want to prolong it, especially since, as I said, all the stakeholders, the government, the social partners, the state of senators of all the clubs, so over the divisions and regardless of their views, have accepted and welcomed this law. I refer to the High House for its adoption and I think that there is no need for further justification here. To conclude, I would just like to say that it will serve, of course, its positive social, economic, financial effects, not only to entrepreneurs, but also to the state. The State is also interested in this, especially in the strategic sectors. In addition, the Act will have a positive impact on the activities of micro, small and medium-sized enterprises, where any cessation of activities There's a lot of trouble involved. We can avoid this if we adopt this amendment. Thank you very much, Marshal.

These savings are the amount of... We compare here over the decade, because this is how the impact assessment of the regulation provides for this. So in the next decade we're talking about the cost of what we have today, or 3 billion 100 million zlotys, or 457 million zlotys, assuming that the program will start to be implemented by the Social Security Office. This is PLN 2.5 billion savings on the cost of operating the program Not on the cost of the program, we don't change the program, we change the way it is paid in 10 years. This is a very significant amount. And we care about public finances... We believe that this is enough reason to make such a change. As a result, one of the successive laws that the High House will deal with will be the Act on Family Care Capital. These are further programmes, the beneficiaries of which are parents, possible among others. That's because we rationalize the expenses that are. Of course, I agree with the fact that local governments were very good at implementing this programme. Nevertheless, this is an operating method that excludes automation, because... Automation is that... Today in the ZUS the program is already being implemented is a good start, it is a very similar program, from a technical point of view similar. It is based on the same solutions and applies to the same group of people. It requires verification with virtually the same data that we have in the case of the 500+ family programme. This is a program that is already being implemented by the ZUS today. Over 90% applications submitted in this programme shall be carried out automatically, without the participation of any official, without the participation of any ZUS employee. That's what automation is all about, and that's why they're saving money. This is possible because applications are submitted electronically and automatically compared with other databases. This is also somewhat the answer to the question of why ZUS does not only deal with pensions. Ladies and gentlemen, the postulate... I don't know if I understand the perspective, Senator Lover's view. From your point of view, would it be optimal to build a second, similar centre, such as the Social Security Office, for the payment of family benefits? I'll apply the analogy to the use of electricity. It's like building a second power plant to run two more outlets. The Social Security Office has had the infrastructure it has built and built for many years, the costs of which are known to you. This isn't cheap infrastructure. Our task, from the point of view of the economy and optimization of the use of resources, is to use existing infrastructure. Here we attach another solution, another task, which from a technical point of view is no different from the previous one. This is the connection of another outlet to a power plant that has huge power generating powers. We just need to continue with this comparison today. Bring the cable so you can connect another socket. And we're gonna hook up another one. Family care capital is another solution that we will be able to implement, based on the same data, on the same systems. So much for the ZUS and how the program is implemented. The text of the Act is contained in print No 499 and the report of the Commission In print No 499 A. ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-07-senat-31-1.u176	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! Report from the Committee on Family, Senior and Social Policy and the Committee on National Economy and Innovation on the Law adopted by the Sejm on 17 September 2021 amending the Act on Trade Reduction on Sundays and holidays and on certain other days. Four amendments were tabled during the discussion at the committee meeting, which were accepted by the High Commissions. The committees ask the Senate to approve the attached draft resolution. Now, the justification. The Act of 10 January 2018 on the restriction of trade on Sundays and holidays and on certain other days entered into force on 1 March 2018. The provisions contained therein provided for the gradual restriction of trade and the execution of trade activities in commercial establishments on Sundays and holidays and on 24 December and on Saturday immediately preceding Easter. Initially, the assessment of the functioning of the provisions of the Act, carried out using the experience of the State Labour Inspection collected during the carrying out of inspection activities related to compliance with the provisions of the Act by traders engaged in commercial activities, indicated a high degree of respect for the provisions of the Act by entrepreneurs. However, the labour inspectors have assessed that certain provisions of the Act do not function in accordance with the intention of the legislator, calling for a number of amendments to be made to the Act. It is worth noting that in the public forum there were less and less frequent voices calling into question the objective pursued by designers of legal solutions that limit the activity of commercial establishments on Sundays and holidays. A comprehensive assessment of the impact of the trade restriction on Sundays by the Ministry of Entrepreneurship and Technology at the beginning of 2019, i.e. one year after the entry into force of the Act, showed that the introduction of trade restriction on Sundays did not have negative effects on retail trade. Not only the positive aspects related to the situation of micro-entrepreneurs and small shops, but also the greater satisfaction of employees and their families, the development of tourist services, accommodation, restaurant and widely understood economy of leisure time were observed. It should be made clear that the restriction of trade on Sundays is a support for many other industries, including tourism, agrotourism, gastronomy, hotels, recreation and culture. Such regulation brings benefits to the entire economy of the country, moreover, this issue also concerns our model of life, i.e. social and cultural issues, even civilization. It is precisely this kind of consideration that determines the trade restrictions already applied in Europe in the countries of the old Union. Unfortunately, the cases of incorrect application of the exemptions provided for in Article have increased over time. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 Act allowing trade and trade-related activities to be carried out on Sundays and holidays. This shall in particular apply to the cases referred to in Article. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 points 6, 7 and 27 of the Act, i.e. the exclusion of trade bans in commercial establishments where the dominant activity consists of trade in newspapers, public transport tickets, tobacco products, tickets for games of chance and betting, in post offices within the meaning of the Law of 23 November 2012, in commercial establishments where trade is carried out by an entrepreneur who is a natural person solely on his own behalf, on his own account. The control activities carried out by labour inspectors show that traders apply these exemptions when the sale of tobacco products is also carried out in their actual commercial establishments, in public food shops or in alcohol sales. In order to benefit from these exemptions, traders change the nature of the dominant activity in CEIDG and in the REGON register, despite the lack of actual grounds for making the changes. It could be caused by a factor... It should be pointed out that the Labour Inspector does not have the authority to examine whether the activities indicated by the entrepreneur as dominant are of such a nature in reality. Consequently, it is not possible to verify whether the sale of the goods in question is indeed dominant. It is not without significance that the dominant activity is defined by the trader for all his activities and that the exemptions concern individual commercial establishments. Following the entry into force of the Act, there was also a problem of avoiding the trade ban on Sundays by the appointment of traders operating commercial establishments, initially mainly grocery stores, to the exclusion referred to in Article. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 point 7 of the Act, i.e. have the status of post within the meaning of the Law of 23 November 2012 on postal law. Over time, this legal gap also began to be used by large commercial networks as well as small entrepreneurs, making trade restrictions on Sundays increasingly fictitious. There is no doubt that, in such cases, there is a breach of the principle of fair competition and intervention becomes necessary. In the case of grocery stores with a signed agency agreement, the courts to which labour inspectors refer applications for punishment shall consider that there has been no breach of the trade ban due to the conclusion by the accused operators of agency contracts with the postal operator, which results in the trader becoming a postal agent and, in the view of the court, it is irrelevant what postal services are involved in the conduct of business. In order to address this problem, statutory intervention was essential. In my opinion, the ban on trade on Sundays is already adopted in Poland and more and more people realise that this civilization leap also affects us, because we are in Europe, we have more and more financial resources in our wallets and this leisure time needs to be used differently. Thank you. [[The meeting is chaired by Deputy Marshal Gabriela Morawska-Stanecka]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-07-senat-31-1.u220	Thank you very much. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! First of all, I would like to thank the joint committees very warmly for their substantive cooperation and for the unanimous adoption of the law without amendments. Similarly, without opposition, this project went through the Sejm, for which we also thank the parliamentarians. I would like to take this opportunity to re-examine the content of the law, because the solutions contained there are very pro-Citizenish. The aim of the Act is to introduce legal bases for the provision of a package of electronic services in the area of civil matters, the common denominator of which is the transactional automation of processes and services using state registers and electronic seals. The most important benefits of the solutions are: facilitating the administration of public affairs, speeding up and facilitating the implementation of cases by the authorities, reducing the cost of financing certain tasks commissioned from the area of civil affairs. The legal solution is, among other things, a solution. a transactional check-in and check-out service carried out in the system without the involvement of an official. At present, the owners of the property must include an electronic version or a scan of a copy from the perpetual book or an extract from Sections I and II of the perpetual book, by registering online. Thanks to the new rules, the e-service will be integrated into the system of perpetual books. Once these changes have been implemented, people who own the property and want to check in online will no longer have to prove that they own the property, the verification will take place automatically without the involvement of the requested officer. This will make the process even faster and more comfortable. A service will be introduced for the collection of a certificate from the civil status register on data processed in the civil status register, accompanied by a qualified electronic stamp of the Minister responsible for Information Technology. Such a document is useful when dealing with many things, e.g. tax, insurance, social or whatever is needed to obtain credit. Such a certificate may be required abroad, inter alia. the employer or social institution. Thanks to the new regulations, it will be easier and quicker to download such a certificate. There will also be a service available to download a certificate from the PESEL register containing a complete or partial copy of the data relating to the person and his/her children using a transactional electronic service. After the entry into force of the new regulations, it will be possible to obtain such a certificate immediately. Currently, it is only possible to check your data in the PESEL register, and this allows you to check your data in the PESEL register. A service will also be introduced to allow for the collection of information about recipients from the Register of Personal Evidence and the PESEL register. At present, obtaining such information is not automated and, despite its request, it requires the participation of an official. The new service will allow you to check events that have occurred within 5 years of their occurrence. What's important, we're the only ones who will be able to find out who's receiving our data or data about the child under our parental authority. That's not gonna happen to anyone on the outside. After the entry into force of the new rules, not only will it be easier to obtain information about the recipients of data, but it will take much longer than today to wait for them, we will get them right away. Another service is the possibility of sealing the Minister's copies of the birth certificates issued ex officio as a result of the electronic notification of the birth of the child. The introduction of such a technical solution will enable the heads of civil status offices to speed up and simplify their work without affecting the credibility of the document itself. The next issue is to ensure that data from the Register of Personal Evidence and the PESEL register can be requested in restricted data transmission mode using a dedicated service and to provide electronic documents confirming compliance with statutory conditions for granting access. Come on. Notification of departure from the country with the intention of permanent residence or return. In the case of a declaration of departure or return of a minor, one of the parents may do so if the PESEL register contains information about the parent's PESEL number of the check-out parent. In view of the very positive assumptions laid down by the law and the earlier support of the committee and the Sejm, I ask the senators to adopt the law. Thank you very much, Mrs. Marshal.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-07-senat-31-1.u222	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The solutions contained in this Act are undoubtedly correct and necessary. The introduction into the legal market of electronic services enabling citizens to directly collect data from public registers is highly expected both by society and by public administrations. It should be expected that these services will reduce the costs of functioning of public administration in Poland. However, attention should be paid to the important fact that all services put in place generally concern citizens. This means that the legislator does not see the purpose of implementing a completely new approach to digitalisation and public services. It should be clarified that the need to take certificates and copies of the civil status certificate is not an objective in itself. This is an instrumental objective that serves to achieve other objectives. Although this e-services will enable the citizen to download documents from the public register more quickly and cheaperly, he will still be forced to mediate the transfer of data or documents from institution A to institution B. A modern approach is to eliminate, where possible, the need for citizens to participate in data exchange processes. A model to be pursued is a situation when a citizen initiates some administrative activity, e.g. submit an application and all or most of the necessary data for the performance of the service will be obtained by the public entity itself. This model, although in the case of copies of the civil status act it has been in operation for more than a year, is not used. It should be stated that only such a model will represent significant progress in the development of e-government in Poland.
