ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-07-senat-31-1.u224	We proceed with the examination of item 8 of the agenda: the Act amending the Foreigners Act and some other laws. The text of the Act is contained in print No 497, and the Commission's reports In the letters Nos 497 A and 497 B. I would like to ask the rapporteur of the Committee on Human Rights, the Rule of Law and Petitions, Senator Michael Sewerynski, to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-07-senat-31-1.u225	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen of the Senators! The subject of the review by the Committee on Human Rights, Laws and Petitions was the amendment of the Act on Foreigners of 12 October 1990, the Law on the Protection of the State Border, and the Act of 2003 on the granting of protection to foreigners on the territory of the Republic of Poland taking into account the current migration situation occurring at the border of the Republic of Poland. The aim is to effectively counter the abuse of international protection institutions by foreigners who, in order to achieve objectives other than protection by persecution or serious harm by submitting an application for international protection, have the right to applicants, including the right to cross the border and stay in the territory of the State, to circumvent the conditions for crossing the external border of the European Union set out in the Schengen Borders Code. The present act is also intended to improve the conduct by the Border Guard authorities of proceedings concerning the illegal crossing of the external border of the European Union by foreigners not seeking international protection, aiming to quickly leave the territory of the Republic of Poland. The amendment introduces a new regulation into the Foreigners Act, according to which, in the case of crossing the external border, within the meaning of the Schengen Code, contrary to the rules of law and the immediate detention of the foreign commander of the Border Guard Office in the framework of the administrative proceedings carried out, he draws up a protocol for crossing the border and issues a decision to leave the territory of the Republic of Poland. The order shall specify the order for the foreigner to leave the territory of the Republic of Poland and shall prohibit the re-entry into the territory of the Republic of Poland and other countries of the Schengen area. It shall also specify the duration of that prohibition as not less than 6 months and not more than 3 years. In addition, the Act provides that the foreigner in respect of whom the provision concerning the prohibition of re-entry into the territory of the Republic of Poland and other Schengen States is to be included in the list of persons whose stay in the territory of the Republic of Poland is undesirable and to be transferred to the Schengen Information System. The Act on the granting of protection to foreigners in the territory of the Republic of Poland introduced a change which allows the Head of the Office to leave without recognition an application for international protection where the application is submitted by the foreigner who is detained immediately after crossing the external border contrary to the rules of law. The Head of Office may not leave without recognition an application made by a foreigner who has arrived directly from the territory in which his life or freedom threatens the danger, persecution or the risk of serious harm, and has provided reliable reasons for illegal entry into the territory of the Republic and has submitted an application for international protection immediately after crossing the border. The amendment also amends the Law on the Protection of the State Border, introducing criminal liability for the action of taking, destroying, damaging, removing, moving or making useless of infrastructure elements located in the border area and intended for the protection of the state border, in particular fences, fences, dams and horns. The legislator provided that the provisions of the Foreigners Act in the existing version apply to proceedings concerning the foreigner's obligation to return initiated and not completed before the date of entry into force of the amendment. With regard to the content of the Commission's Law, the Legislative Office raised reservations that some of the provisions of the Law were incompatible with the Charter of Fundamental Rights of the European Union, with the case law of the Court of Justice of the European Union and with the Geneva Convention on the Status of Refugees in 1951. In view of the reservations I mentioned, two proposals were made in the course of the discussion: the proposal to reject the law as far as possible, i.e. the proposal to reject it, and the proposal to adopt the amendment act. It is about 2 amendments, which are purely technical and legislative in nature, to add the word 'or 'where it is missing ', we add them because of the correct syntax 'and the separation of criminal rules in a separate chapter. Most of the votes were adopted with a proposal to support the bill with two amendments attached. A motion to reject the law has not been accepted, but it will be presented with the support of minorities. That's it, Mr. Marshal. Thank you for your vote.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-07-senat-31-1.u226	Thank you very much, Mr. Marshal. And now I'm gonna ask for minority proposals. I would like to ask the minority rapporteur of the Committee on Human Rights, the Laws and Petitions, Senator Gabriela Moravian-Stanecka, to present a proposal for a minority of committees.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-07-senat-31-1.u227	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the motion of the minority committee. This is a motion to reject this bill. I will not describe the provisions of this law because they have been described by the Senator-Rapporteur of the committee. I would just like to point out why, for what reasons, a minority of committees have applied to reject the law. Maybe we could start by justifying this bill. The explanatory memorandum of the Act states that the proposed legislation is intended to allow for better conduct of proceedings on illegal border crossings and to promote security and public order protection. There was a massive influx into Europe of migrants, many of whom were radicalized representatives of many cultures, religions, and even extremists, and I quote the justification. The explanatory memorandum also mentions the abuse of refugee procedures by economic migrants. However, it does not contain any specific information from which it can be concluded that foreigners applying for protection actually pose a threat. Of course, I will not refer to the famous press conference of the Minister of the Interior, where the origin of the paintings presented at this conference was not the Polish-Belarusian border and they were not from that period, they were registered much earlier. Therefore, the arguments set out in the explanatory memorandum of the law, namely the need to prevent irregular migration, the abuse of refugee procedures, are not really covered. However, the Act should be assessed for its compliance with the provisions of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, European Union law, Council of Europe law, international refugee law, the Geneva Convention, and OSCE obligations. Regardless of the fact that the Belarusian authorities, of course intentionally, make it easier for foreigners to arrive at the external border of the European Union, Poland is obliged to protect its border to comply with international law and European Union law, also because it is the external border of the European Union. According to these provisions, border control and border protection measures against return of foreigners are required to comply with European Union law, including the Charter of Fundamental Rights of the European Union, international refugee law, the European Convention on Human Rights, the International Covenant on Civil and Political Rights, the European Union Directive on common procedures for granting and withdrawing international protection of 2013, the OSCE's obligations, starting with the Helsinki Final Act of 1975, and the Final Document of the Vienna KBWE Conference, 1989, and the principle of non-refoulement. Foreigners have the right to have an application for international protection lodged at the border or on the territory of a Member State of the European Union examined and Member States are required to allow such applicants to remain in their territory pending the examination of the application. The foreigner cannot be expelled from the territory of the Republic of Poland without an individual examination of his situation, and the likelihood of violating his fundamental rights in the event of expulsion, including in particular the prohibition of torture, must result in such expulsion. Leaving the application for international protection without recognition is only possible in specific cases. They do not cross the border against the rules of law. If one of these conditions is the arrival from the so-called. a safe third country, but European Union law requires that the grounds for declaring the country concerned safe be specified in detail. At this point I would like to remind you that the European Court of Human Rights has recognised that Belarus does not have an effective asylum system, and reports that Belarusian services force foreigners to cross the border with Poland and prevent them from returning, show that Belarus cannot be regarded as a safe country. This is from the justification of the M.K. case. against Poland. Changes to the Foreigners Act, which violates these provisions, are primarily discussed earlier the introduction of a new type of administrative decision issued to the foreigner. - Orders to cross the border against the rules of law. This is a different kind of decision than the decision to make a commitment to return. Order issued in the situation of detention of a foreigner immediately after the illegal crossing of the external border of the European Union will indicate the return of the foreigner from the territory of the Republic of Poland, include a ban on re-entry into the territory of the Republic of Poland and other countries of the Schengen area, and consequently the issuing of this prohibition The entry in the national list of foreigners whose stay is undesirable and in the Schengen Information System for the purpose of refusing entry. This order is intended to be absolute, i.e. unconditional, so it can be issued and executed despite the application by the foreigner for international protection and without taking into account other circumstances on the part of the foreigner, which should result in the failure of such an arrangement. In other words, making an application for international protection will not affect the issue and implementation of that order. It will, of course, be immediately feasible, i.e. the lodging of a complaint will not affect the performance. So the foreigner will simply be returned to the external border. There is no regulation concerning the basis for compulsory enforcement of the order, the indication of the country to which the foreigner is to be transferred, the issuing of travel documents to the foreigner, as is the case with the decision to leave the country where the country from which he came refuses to accept it. This is incompatible with the European Union's asylum law because, as provided for in Directive 2013/32/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council, a foreigner who has applied for international protection is an applicant. This is the first step in the international protection procedure. And the provisions of the Directive provide that applicants are permitted to remain in a Member State only for the purposes of the procedure until the determining authority has taken a decision. The European Court of Human Rights confirmed this in its case law: it is necessary to ensure that the return of the country of origin is protected until the application is properly processed. The provisions of the Act therefore infringe the provisions of the Directive and the right to asylum contained in Article. 18 The Charter of Fundamental Rights of the European Union, and if expulsion were to take place in a territory where life or freedom would endanger us, we would also be in breach of the principle of non-refoulement contained in Article. Article 33 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Geneva Convention on the Status of Refugees. In accordance with the proposed rules, there will be no individualised examination of the consequences of enforcement, i.e. the threat of torture and persecution, which could lead to violations of international law and the principles arising from the case law of the European Court of Human Rights. A foreigner must not be expelled if it involves a real risk of violating the prohibition of torture or cruel, inhuman or degrading treatment and punishment. This principle is absolute and there are no exceptions to it in international law. Those provisions are also to apply where the foreigner is detained immediately after crossing the external border contrary to the provisions of the law, which shows that the removal and enforcement of the order falls within the scope of the control of the external border of the European Union governed by the provisions of the Schengen Borders Code. It's an art. Article 2 (10) to (12) and Article 2 (2) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013. Article 13 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation In turn, in the article. The Schengen Borders Code 3 and 4 explicitly states that Member States fully comply with, inter alia, the provisions of the Schengen Borders Code.: Charter of Fundamental Rights of the European Union, international law, including the Geneva Convention, obligations relating to access to international protection, and in particular the principles non-refoulement and fundamental rights. The Schengen Borders Code also indicates that decisions are taken individually and Article. Article 13 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of that Code provides that a person who has crossed the border contrary to the rules of law shall be detained and subjected to a procedure complying with the requirements of Directive 2008/115/EC governing the standards and procedures applied by Member States with regard to the return of irregular migrants. This law also infringes the right to an effective remedy guaranteed by Article. 13 Directive 2008/115/EC and the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union which provides that, in the event of a threat to a foreigner's death, torture or persecution, the lodging of an appeal should suspend the implementation of the decision. The directive gives legal advice, legal representation and assistance to the translator. Well, and the complaint to the chief doesn't meet the requirements of proper instant control, because it's an instant control of the body of the same formation. As regards the entry of data into the Schengen Information System, according to the Regulation the data of the foreigner in the list of foreigners whose stay in the territory of the Republic of Poland is undesirable, and in the Schengen Information System for the purpose of refusing entry This is a Regulation of Parliament and of the Council concerning the Schengen Information System II... entry into such a system for refusal of entry or stay shall be entered on the basis of an entry into the national system but resulting from a decision taken on the basis of an individual assessment by the competent administrative authorities or courts in accordance with the procedural rules laid down in national law. So that's not enough. And of course, this law also contains provisions on changes to the law on granting protection to foreigners. The project introduced the possibility of leaving without recognition an application for international protection when it was lodged by a foreigner detained immediately after crossing the external border of the European Union contrary to the rules of law, unless the foreigner came from the territory in which he was in danger, presented the reasons for non-compliance with the provisions of the border crossing and submitted the application immediately after it was crossed. The proposed solution refers to 2 separate institutions in Directive 2013/32/EU, i.e. the identification of the application in an accelerated manner and the recognition of the application as inadmissible. As far as the accelerated procedure is concerned, under the directive, crossing the border contrary to the rules and not submitting an application without delay only gives the possibility to carry out an accelerated procedure, i.e. a substantive examination of the application and not to leave it unrecognized. The decision on the application must be made after individual, objective and impartial consideration and after a precise and up-to-date analysis of the situation in the country of origin. In addition, Article. Article 10 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Directive 2013/32/EU provides that an application for protection may not be rejected or left undiagnosed solely because of failure to comply with it as soon as possible. In view of the fact that such a short law, because it is a very short law, contains so many inconsistencies with all international agreements signed by Poland, also with those provisions which regulate the protection of the external border of the European Union, and above all with the Geneva Convention and with the Polish Constitution, with provisions on the possibility of asylum, a minority of committees requests that this law be rejected in its entirety.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-07-senat-31-1.u280	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen of the Senators! On behalf of the Joint Committees, the Legislative Committee and the Committee on Human Rights, the Rule of Law and Petitions, I would like to present a report on the review of the amendment of the Law The bill deals with matters related to the reduction of widely understood corruption phenomena, in this project there are a number of solutions such as. the deprivation of public rights of persons convicted of corruption, the prohibition of such persons from applying for public money under tenders or the prohibition of employment in companies where the Treasury or local authorities have at least 10% shares or shares, local governments and parliamentarians, or as part of an increase in the standard of public disclosure imposed on political parties by the obligation to keep and update registers of contributions and to place them in the Public Information Bulletin. Ladies and gentlemen, the joint committees in the course of their work at their meeting on 21 September have introduced a number of amendments, after having heard the voices of the experts who have prepared opinions for this project. We could read the opinions of Professor Ryszard Piotrowski, Professor Arthur Nowak-Far and Dr. Marcin Walecki. These opinions assessed the compatibility of the proposed regulations with the provisions of national law, including the basic law, i.e. the Constitution, the provisions of Community law, which bind Poland as a Member State of the European Union, or with recommendations from bodies such as GRECO or the Council of Europe, institutions specialised in reducing corruption and raising standards of public disclosure. In the course of the work, the committee accepted amendments tabled by Senators Bury, Kwiatkowski and Szejnfeld. Among these amendments are the following. The first is an amendment which, without denying the appropriateness of introducing a new criminal measure in the criminal code, in the form of a ban on the occupation of posts or the performance of work or the provision of services to public authorities and public institutions in the context of those who infringe the rules on the restriction of corruption, introduced the principle that the measure to be applied in the case of particularly outrageous corruption and official offences will be a measure to be applied by the court on the basis of the optional application of the criminal measure and not the obligation to apply it, i.e. the court will decide whether or not to apply it. Discretion in the decision on penalties and criminal measures in the assessment of applicants This position was supported by the members of the Joint Committee It is one of the most important manifestations of the constitutional principle of judicial independence and the principle of jurisdictional independence resulting from Article. 8 of the Code of Criminal Procedure, which is one of the key principles of the criminal trial. If the original solution had been adopted, in practice the court would have been brought into the machinery for the application of the rules, nothing would have been left to the judge's decision on the criminal measure to be applied in this respect. The second amendment was the one which, according to the original submission, introduced a new ban on committing crimes against the perpetrator... Unfortunately, this later did not find support at the level of the Parliament's work, but by decision of the members of the joint committees we restored the appropriate prohibition of the official crime described in the article. 231, which, as I emphasized, was originally in the presentation. The third amendment, which the joint committees considered appropriate to support, is an amendment which deletes the provision added as paragraph. 10, excluding from the rules of disclosure payments below the amount of PLN 10 000 and membership fees. We considered that the members of the Joint Committee should be disclosed. The fourth amendment, which created the greatest emotions especially at the stage of the Parliamentary work, was an amendment concerning when restrictions should enter for Members and Senators in the provision of work in public institutions and companies of the State Treasury. In line with the decision made by the parliamentarians at the stage of the Sejm's work, this restriction was to enter from the next parliamentary term. As the Legislative Bureau pointed out, if the initiators of the bill were to propose raising the standards of parliamentarians and stigmatizing the further-reaching politicisation of e.g. in the companies of the Treasury, they would have allowed this conflict and these negative phenomena to exist consciously until the end of their term of office, it would have been completely incomprehensible to the public. So we say that this kind of situation, that parliamentarians work in companies of the Treasury, is a pathological situation; the legislator sees the need to regulate and ban such a merger, while at the same time making an informed decision that these rules will only enter into force in over two years. Hence the decision of the joint committees to bring these rules into force more quickly. In practice, this provision would enter into force as would the rest of the amendment provisions, i.e. on 1 January 2022. Members of the Joint Committee considered it sufficient time for the parties concerned to adapt to the new wording. Otherwise, there might be a suspicion that the parliamentary majority adopting these rules today wants to protect its interests, the interests of those who combine the role of parliamentarian with another function, which the legislator in this act clearly considers negatively. It goes on for example. for employment in a company of the Treasury. Removal of the article. 11 'cause that's what this amendment is all about. It is also in the opinion of the Legislative Office that there is no reason for the provision of the law introducing the principle of incoherence between Member and Senator positions and a number of other public functions to enter into force later than the rest of the legislation. Ladies and gentlemen, the vast majority of the amendments were adopted by a large majority of votes, sometimes even without opposition. Sometimes somebody held off. I'm glad we could work on this bill in such a consistent way. At the same time, parliamentarians in the course of the joint committees' legislative work pointed out that they did not exclude that further amendments would be tabled in plenary, e.g. in the context of the register of contracts. But, of course, it is up to you and the senators to decide whether such amendments will be tabled. For my part, on behalf of the Joint Committees, I recommend that the High Senate adopt the Act in print No 495 in accordance with the conclusions of the Joint Committee meeting held on 6 October this year. ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-07-senat-31-1.u288	Thank you very much. Senator, for the constitutional protection of acquired rights. We don't change the rules of the game in its course. This is evident from the judgments of the Constitutional Court, because the principle of protection of acquired rights is linked to the principle of citizen's confidence in the State and its law. The legislator must not set traps for citizens, surprise them, create legal situations during a specific legal relationship that would introduce new solutions to those that occurred before the legal relationship was established. Let me give you an example: judgment of the Constitutional Court of 16 June 2003 K 52/02. The Court dealt with the request of the "Solidarity' concerning the equalisation of workers appointed on the basis of appointments with others whose employment relationship is precisely the result of the employment contract and concluded that the amendment of the rules of the game during the duration of the employment relationship would be in breach of the principle of citizen's trust in the State or the protection of acquired rights linked to the fundamental principle of the democratic rule of law referred to in Article. 2. So that the principles underlying such regulation are very important. And in addition, we vote for the voters. It is the voters who will decide whether they want to or not to choose specific representatives who have these and not other additional places of employment. So let's give the vote to democracy and the constitution. That's all. Thank you.

The caselaw here is clear: you cannot change the rules of the game during it. It's like all of a sudden, during the game, allowing 10 players to be replaced on the field.

It concerns mainly Regulation 2019/103, which will apply from 31 December this year. This regulation has made significant changes to the provisions on common basic standards for civil aviation security. It concerns the background check of persons carrying out civil aviation security tasks. The Act sets out the scope of activities undertaken in the framework of standard and extended background checks, categories of persons subject to this verification, entities responsible for this verification, and the frequency of these checks. It sounds a little complicated, ladies and gentlemen, but that is the language, say, of European laws, of the European Commission. The text of the act is contained in print No 503, and the report of the committee In print No 503 A. I would like to ask the rapporteur of the Committee on Infrastructure and the Committee on Territorial Government and State Administration, Senator Janusz Bęcherz, to present a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-07-senat-31-1.u343	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I don't think it's gonna be that fast now. I have the honour to present a report to the Joint Committees, the Committee on Territorial Government and State Administration and the Committee on Infrastructure, on the Law adopted by the Sejm on the amendment of the Law of Construction and the Law on Planning and Spatial Development; Parliamentary Printing No. 1534, Senate Printing No.503. The Senate Marshal sent the bill to these committees. The joint committee meeting was held yesterday. After considering the bill, the joint committees ask the High Senate to approve the attached draft resolution. The main objective and the need to amend the Law of 7 July 1994 is that construction law must provide simple legal solutions to enable the construction of single-family homes in order to meet their own housing needs. The Act introduces the possibility of building single-family houses without obtaining a building permit, without the establishment of a construction manager and without keeping a construction log in the so-called procedure. applications with a construction project. In the absence of a local spatial planning plan, a decision on the conditions of construction is envisaged, so-called. wousts, for these types of objects within 21 days. The object of the application is to be a building with an area of up to 70 m2. Although these objects are supposed to be small and simple, they are allowed to be no more than two floors. The Act assumes that houses built with an area of up to 70 m2 will be freestanding buildings, and determines the permissible density of buildings, indicating that the number of these buildings cannot be greater than 1 per 500 m2 of plot area. An investor will be required to accompany the declaration of freestanding, no more than two-storey single-family residential buildings up to 70 m2 with a declaration made under criminal liability that the planned construction is carried out in order to meet his own housing needs and that he accepts responsibility for the management of the construction in the event of an undetermined construction manager. The body of the architectural and construction administration shall, for a period of not less than 30 days and not more than 60 days, include relevant information on the public information bulletin on the subject-matter side of the office serving it. In addition, building houses up to 70 m2 to meet their own housing needs will require notification of the building supervisory authorities and the designer supervising the conformity of the construction implementation with the project with the intended date of commencement of work. The investor will also be obliged to require geodetic land designation and, after building a house, to carry out a geodetic post-executive inventory. The Act provides for differentiated requirements for the construction of a house with an area of up to 70 m2 and an individual recreation building, with an increase of up to 70 m2 of the permissible building area of freestanding ground-floor individual recreation buildings with a width of not more than 6 m and an extension of brackets limited to 2 m. The draft law discussed today was introduced by the Council of Ministers on 8 September 2021. "Sejm printing, as I said earlier, is nr. 1534. Its first reading took place in the Committee on Infrastructure and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy. At second reading, Members submitted a motion to reject the draft law in its entirety and 11 amendments, but both the rejection proposal and the amendments were not supported in the third reading. The Sejm passed the bill on September 17th. 248 Members voted in favour, 178 against and 20 abstentions. The draft law was subject to public consultation, but the local government party regrets that the government party did not wait for the opinion of the Joint Government and the Territorial Government Commission and addressed the draft law to the Sejm, in breach of the standards of cooperation and consultation between the government and the local government. At the same time, the local government pointed out that the project planned for the process contained such far-reaching changes compared to the project made available in the context of the public consultation and the opinion that it was essentially a new project and that it should be re-examined and consulted as such. These differences between projects are mentioned by the social side as much as 15 and these differences are quite serious. The joint meeting of the Joint Committees was attended by inter alia. Piotr Uściński, Secretary of State at the Ministry of Development and Technology, together with her colleagues, Mrs Dorota Cabańska, acting as Chief Inspector of Construction Supervision, with colleagues, and Mr Leszek Korczak from the Department of Infrastructure of the Supreme Audit Chamber. Senators Zygmunt Frankiewicz, Wadim Tyszkiewicz, Janusz Pęcherz, and Andrzej Porawski, director of the office of the Polish Towns Association, Adam Korgul, representative of Warmia-Mazurski communes and powiats, who presented both the position of the municipalities and districts, as well as the position of the Board of the Silesian Association of Municipalities and Powiatów, President of the National Council of the Polish Chamber of Construction Engineers, representative of the Chamber of Architects of the Republic of Poland, representative of the Polish Municipal Union, representative of the Polish County Union, expert in spatial planning Ryszard Kowalski and Michał Olszewski, deputy president of Warsaw. The main problems raised in the discussion were issues that were repeated practically in the speeches, but they are important enough that I will mention some of these main problems. Firstly, according to the solutions proposed, the investor will have to take on the criminal liability which so far rested on the construction manager with adequate education, experience and above all insurance against civil liability in the event of errors or disasters, which, of course, the private investor will not have. Here there were various estimates that the savings resulting from the lack of employment of the construction manager would be actually small. The various calculations provide for a reduction in costs in this respect in the order of 0.5% up to a maximum of 2%. That's 2%. It's even a little exaggerated. This law opens the door to home setting virtually without any control. In fact, it establishes that these houses can only be built for the investor's own housing needs, on the basis of a statement. But how will it be verified? Who's gonna stop selling a house like this in a year, two years or five years after they built it? Who's gonna check if the house is really 70 m2? Although here a representative of the Ministry or Minister explained that it would be verifiable, there will certainly be a lot of problems with verification. This is a big legal gap, beneficial for investors who want to make money quickly. Those were the suggestions. It should be pointed out that the registration of such a house of up to 70 m2 must be accompanied by practically the same number of documents as in the case of a building permit decision, and construction may take place even on the date of submission of the documentation. And the question is: will the architectural and construction body have the opportunity to read this documentation, or, all the more so, will it be able to respond in the event of a lack of documentation? The result of this law, as they have pointed out, will be architectural chaos, just as is happening today in gardens where changes... arise as a result of such actions of various types of architectural creations and the like. Of course, there were other serious reservations, among others. such that there is no possibility for the Authority to object even in a situation of conflict of law. I'm not gonna go into detail here, I'm just gonna exchange some basic notes. Another remark is the imposition of a penalty for exceeding the deadline for issuing decisions on housing conditions and shortening the time of agreement and silent consent. Here, imposing a penalty for exceeding the deadline... A new 90-day deadline was introduced when deciding on the construction conditions for all types of objects. This is a major revision of the legislation, which is not linked to the basic objective of the proposed amendment of the legislation, which is justified by the need to provide simple legal tools to enable the construction of single-family homes to meet their own housing needs. This change leads to a clear priority decision on the construction conditions in relation to local land use plans. In turn, shortening the deadline for issuing decisions on housing conditions for houses, which we are mainly talking about here, i.e. houses up to 70 m2 of the area, only up to 21 days and time of arrangement up to 7 days... According to the people here, this is actually impossible in practice. Another amendment is the introduction of new rules for recreational buildings. As I mentioned earlier, this is a change in the permissible area of buildings of freestanding buildings. The proposed change here assumes that we could build a house of up to 70 m2, which would be a one-storey house. According to experts, it would be easier and safer to build a building that has 2 floors than an object that has 1 floor and 70 m2 buildings. I would like to draw attention to a few more elements that have been raised. When the control of the architectural and construction administrations is excluded, the responsibility for the investor's compliance with the laws and regulations should rest with the construction supervisory authorities. It has been pointed out that, especially in the departments of the service, although they are very, very well prepared in substance, they are so severely weakened that they are unable to meet these requirements. Another thing that has been pointed out is the 70-metre recreational building, where there is a width of construction elements up to 6 m and a height of brackets up to 2 m. This provision can also create another area for abuse, as it will allow for easy circumvention of both building law and the law on spatial planning and development. It's a matter of 21 days and punishment. This is a serious reservation from all interested parties, especially the representatives of the counties and municipalities, who believe that in the current situation the state of employment in the offices is insufficient, that they will often not be able to make such a decision within 21 days. This is related to the penalty of PLN 500 per day of delay if this is reported by the investor concerned. These are the most serious things that have been raised. There were 10 votes in favour of the motion to reject the bill, six senators were against. The motion to reject the bill has been voted out. Thank you for your attention.

We proceed with the examination of item 13 of the agenda: the Act on the ratification of the European Convention for the Protection of Audiovisual Heritage, drawn up at Strasbourg on 8 November 2001. ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-07-senat-31-1.u367	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Senate Committee on Foreign Affairs and the European Union a report on the Act on the ratification of the European Convention for the Protection of Audiovisual Heritage, drawn up in Strasbourg on 8 November 2001, on which the committee was sitting at its 80th second meeting on 6 October 2001. The aim of the Act is to agree to the ratification by the President of the Republic of Poland of the European Convention for the Protection of Audiovisual Heritage. The purpose of the Convention, as enshrined in its Article. 1, is to ensure the protection of the European audiovisual heritage and its recognition both as an art form and as a record of the past, by collecting, preserving and making available film material for cultural, scientific and research purposes in the public interest. The essential obligations imposed by the Convention are the introduction of a statutory obligation to collect and store film material produced or co-produced in the territory of the State of the Party. It's an article that talks about it. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the and an indication of one or more bodies whose task will be to perform the functions of the national archive, as stated in the article. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 convention... [[Talking in the O.R.]] [[Minister, gentlemen... the senators, the minister, either I invite you to cocksuckers, or I ask for silence.]] The obligation to set up state archiving bodies is complemented by the need for the State Party to implement the provisions which allow the public authority to carry out supervision in practice on the activities of the established archive, says the article. Article 6 (6) (a) 3 of the Convention and require the Authority to provide the technical and financial means necessary to carry out the archival activities. As highlighted in part in the explanatory memorandum, but also in the opinion of the Senate Legislative Office, in the Polish legal system the obligations imposed by the Convention are already fulfilled, in particular on the basis of the provisions of the Act of 30 June 2005 on cinematography. On the other hand, the duties of the archiving body are full of the National Film Archives and Audiovisual Institute. The explanatory memorandum of the bill states that the adoption of the Convention into the Polish legal order will promote the harmonisation of standards for the transmission and storage of audiovisual works, including cinematographic works, also at international level. The binding of the Republic of Poland to the Convention will allow to supplement accepted international agreements on the protection of audiovisual heritage. In addition, it has been pointed out that ratification of the Convention will not result in the need to amend the existing rules as well as the financial implications for the State budget. The Convention concerns matters governed by the Law and is therefore subject to ratification with the prior consent of the Act, in accordance with Article. Article 89 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 point 5 of the Constitution. The Act is to enter into force 14 days after the date of the announcement. In favour of the bill, 432 Members voted against, 1 against, 10 abstentions. The Committee on Foreign Affairs and the European Union, at its eighty-second meeting on 6 October this year, following the presentation by the Deputy Minister for Foreign Affairs, Mr Paweł Jabłoński, without votes in the discussion, adopted the draft ratification act unanimously. Therefore, on behalf of the Senate Committee on Foreign Affairs and the European Union, I request the adoption of the Act on the ratification of the European Convention for the Protection of Audiovisual Heritage, drawn up in Strasbourg on 8 November 2001. Thank you very much.
